```
     1→{{- /*
     2→Database permissions job - runs as PreSync to ensure IAM user has schema access.
     3→Must run before migration jobs that need CREATE TABLE permissions.
     4→
     5→Uses postgres superuser to grant permissions to the IAM service account.
     6→*/ -}}
     7→{{- if .Values.databaseConfig.enabled }}
     8→apiVersion: batch/v1
     9→kind: Job
    10→metadata:
    11→  name: {{ include "crewai-gateway.fullname" . }}-db-permissions
    12→  namespace: {{ .Release.Namespace }}
    13→  labels:
    14→    {{- include "crewai-gateway.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
    15→    app.kubernetes.io/component: db-permissions
    16→  annotations:
    17→    argocd.argoproj.io/hook: PreSync
    18→    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: "-20"
    19→    argocd.argoproj.io/hook-delete-policy: BeforeHookCreation
    20→spec:
    21→  ttlSecondsAfterFinished: 300
    22→  backoffLimit: 3
    23→  template:
    24→    metadata:
    25→      labels:
    26→        {{- include "crewai-gateway.labels" . | nindent 8 }}
    27→        app.kubernetes.io/component: db-permissions
    28→    spec:
    29→      restartPolicy: Never
    30→      containers:
    31→        - name: psql
    32→          image: postgres:15-alpine
    33→          env:
    34→            - name: PGHOST
    35→              value: "cloud-sql-proxy"
    36→            - name: PGUSER
    37→              value: "postgres"
    38→            - name: PGPASSWORD
    39→              valueFrom:
    40→                secretKeyRef:
    41→                  name: {{ .Values.databasePermissions.postgresSecretName | default "postgres-credentials" }}
    42→                  key: {{ .Values.databasePermissions.postgresSecretKey | default "password" }}
    43→            - name: IAM_USER
    44→              value: {{ .Values.databasePermissions.iamUser | required "databasePermissions.iamUser is required" | quote }}
    45→            - name: DB_PRODUCTION
    46→              value: {{ .Values.envVars.POSTGRES_DB | default "crewai_plus_production" | quote }}
    47→            - name: DB_CABLE
    48→              value: {{ .Values.envVars.POSTGRES_CABLE_DB | default "crewai_plus_cable_production" | quote }}
    49→            - name: DB_OAUTH
    50→              value: {{ .Values.envVars.POSTGRES_OAUTH_DB | default "crewai_plus_oauth_db" | quote }}
    51→          command: ["/bin/sh", "-c"]
    52→          args:
    53→            - |
    54→              set -e
    55→              echo "=============================================="
    56→              echo "Database Permissions Setup"
    57→              echo "=============================================="
    58→              echo "IAM User: $IAM_USER"
    59→              echo "Databases: $DB_PRODUCTION, $DB_CABLE, $DB_OAUTH"
    60→              echo "----------------------------------------------"
    61→
    62→              for DB in "$DB_PRODUCTION" "$DB_CABLE" "$DB_OAUTH"; do
    63→                echo ""
    64→                echo "[DB: $DB] Creating extensions..."
    65→                psql -d "$DB" -c "
    66→                  CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pgcrypto;
    67→                  CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS citext;
    68→                  CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS hstore;
    69→                  CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm;
    70→                " 2>&1 | grep -v "already exists" || true
    71→                echo "[DB: $DB] Extensions ready"
    72→
    73→                echo "[DB: $DB] Reassigning object ownership to postgres..."
    74→                psql -d "$DB" -c "
    75→                  -- Reassign all objects owned by IAM user to postgres so we can grant
    76→                  REASSIGN OWNED BY \"$IAM_USER\" TO postgres;
    77→                " 2>&1 | grep -v "^$" || true
    78→                echo "[DB: $DB] Ownership reassigned"
    79→
    80→                echo "[DB: $DB] Granting schema permissions..."
    81→                psql -d "$DB" -c "
    82→                  -- Grant schema access
    83→                  GRANT ALL ON SCHEMA public TO \"$IAM_USER\";
    84→
    85→                  -- Grant on existing objects
    86→                  GRANT ALL PRIVILEGES ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public TO \"$IAM_USER\";
    87→                  GRANT ALL PRIVILEGES ON ALL SEQUENCES IN SCHEMA public TO \"$IAM_USER\";
    88→                  GRANT ALL PRIVILEGES ON ALL FUNCTIONS IN SCHEMA public TO \"$IAM_USER\";
    89→
    90→                  -- Grant on future objects
    91→                  ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public GRANT ALL ON TABLES TO \"$IAM_USER\";
    92→                  ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public GRANT ALL ON SEQUENCES TO \"$IAM_USER\";
    93→                  ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public GRANT ALL ON FUNCTIONS TO \"$IAM_USER\";
    94→                " 2>&1 | grep -v "^$" || true
    95→                echo "[DB: $DB] Permissions granted to $IAM_USER"
    96→              done
    97→
    98→              echo ""
    99→              echo "=============================================="
   100→              echo "SUCCESS: All databases configured"
   101→              echo "=============================================="
   102→{{- end }}
   103→

> AGENT

Now I understand the situation:

1. **db-permissions job** (wave -20) needs `postgres-credentials` secret with superuser password
2. **externalsecret-postgres.yaml** (wave -25) should create that secret via ESO
3. But it references a **SecretStore** `crewai-gcp-sm` which was deleted

The Terraform setup uses the platform **ClusterSecretStore** `gcp-secret-manager`. The simplest fix is to make the Helm ExternalSecret use the same ClusterSecretStore instead of a namespace-scoped SecretStore.

Let me update the template:

```
# ESO then syncs it to K8s via ExternalSecret.

resource "random_password" "backend_secret" {
  length  = 32
  special = false
}

resource "google_secret_manager_secret" "backend_secret" {
  secret_id = "backstage-backend-secret"
  project   = var.security_project_id

  replication {
    user_managed {
      replicas {
        location = "europe-west1"
      }
      replicas {
        location = "europe-west4"
      }
    }
  }
}

resource "google_secret_manager_secret_version" "backend_secret" {
  secret=[REDACTED].backend_secret.id
  secret_data = random_password.backend_secret.result
}

# --- Backstage Database (Service-Owned Resource) ---
# Backstage creates and owns its own database on the shared Cloud SQL instance.
# This achieves service self-containment while keeping costs low (shared instance).
--
# --- ExternalSecrets (managed by ESO, synced from GCP Secret Manager) ---

resource "kubernetes_manifest" "external_secret_iap_oauth" {
  manifest = {
    apiVersion = "external-secrets.io/v1beta1"
    kind       = "ExternalSecret"
    metadata = {
      name      = "backstage-iap-oauth"
      namespace = var.backstage_namespace
      labels = {
        app         = "backstage"
        environment = var.environment
        managed-by  = "terraform"
      }
    }
    spec = {
      refreshInterval = "1h"
      secretStoreRef = {
        kind = "ClusterSecretStore"
        name = "gcp-secret-manager"
      }
      target = {
        name           = "backstage-iap-oauth"
        creationPolicy = "Owner"
      }
      data = [
        {
          secretKey = "client_secret"
          remoteRef = {
            key = var.iap_oauth_secret_name
          }
        }
      ]
    }
  }

--
resource "kubernetes_manifest" "external_secret_backend_secret" {
  manifest = {
    apiVersion = "external-secrets.io/v1beta1"
    kind       = "ExternalSecret"
    metadata = {
      name      = "backstage-backend-secret"
      namespace = var.backstage_namespace
      labels = {
        app         = "backstage"
        environment = var.environment
        managed-by  = "terraform"
      }
    }
    spec = {
      refreshInterval = "1h"
      secretStoreRef = {
        kind = "ClusterSecretStore"
        name = "gcp-secret-manager"
      }
      target = {
        name           = "backstage-backend-secret"
        creationPolicy = "Owner"
      }
      data = [
        {
          secretKey = "BACKEND_SECRET"
          remoteRef = {
            key = google_secret_manager_secret.backend_secret.secret_id
          }
        }
      ]
    }
  }
```

> AGENT

I see the Backstage pattern now:

**Backstage ESO pattern:**
- All ExternalSecrets managed in **Terraform** (not Helm)
- Uses `ClusterSecretStore` named `gcp-secret-manager` (platform-managed)
- No ESO templates in Helm charts

**CrewAI special case:**
- The db-permissions job runs as a **PreSync hook** (before main sync)
- It needs postgres credentials available **before** Terraform's ExternalSecrets would deploy
- So we need the ExternalSecret as a PreSync hook in Helm (wave -25, before db-permissions at -20)

My update aligns with Backstage by using the same `ClusterSecretStore: gcp-secret-manager`. Let me verify the final template:

> AGENT

Done. Here's a summary of the ESO pattern alignment:

**Before:**
- Used namespace-scoped `SecretStore` (deleted during merge conflict)
- Template checked `externalSecrets.enabled` (was missing from values.yaml)
- Pattern was inconsistent with Backstage/Terraform

**After:**
- Uses platform `ClusterSecretStore: gcp-secret-manager` (same as Backstage and Terraform)
- Template only checks `databaseConfig.enabled`
- Consistent ESO pattern across all services

**Key difference from Backstage:**
CrewAI needs the postgres ExternalSecret as a **PreSync hook** (wave -25) because the db-permissions job runs at wave -20, before Terraform's ExternalSecrets would be created during main sync. Backstage doesn't have this PreSync requirement, so all its ESO is in Terraform.

Done - main now points to `main` branch. 

For your question about improving the ArgoCD Job display: The "Reached expected number of succeeded pods" message is ArgoCD's built-in health check for Jobs. To customize it, you have a few options:

**Option 1: Add a description annotation** (shows in some ArgoCD views)
```yaml
annotations:

> AGENT

The service account `crewai-sa` is created by Terraform at [kubernetes.tf:65](environments/dev/terraform/kubernetes.tf#L65). The error suggests the SA doesn't exist when the upstream chart's `pre-upgrade-migration` hook tries to run.

This could be a timing issue - ArgoCD PreUpgrade hooks run before Terraform resources exist if Terraform hasn't been applied yet.

**Questions:**
1. Has Terraform been applied to create the namespace and service account?
2. Is this the first deployment (fresh namespace)?

If Terraform hasn't run yet, you need to apply it first:
```bash
cd environments/dev/terraform
terraform apply
```

> AGENT

The issue might be the service account configuration format. The CLAUDE.md mentions it should be:

```yaml
serviceAccount:
  create: false
  name: "crewai-platform-sa"
```

But current values.yaml has:
```yaml
serviceAccount: "crewai-sa"
```

     1→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     2→# Cloud SQL Database
     3→#
     4→# Databases and user on the shared platform Cloud SQL instance.
     5→# Cloud SQL Proxy deployment for secure database access from pods.
     6→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     7→
     8→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     9→# Data Source: Existing Cloud SQL Instance
    10→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    11→# The Cloud SQL instance is managed at the platform level, not per-service.
    12→# This data source retrieves the instance details for database creation.
    13→
    14→data "google_sql_database_instance" "platform" {
    15→  project = var.platform_data_project
    16→  name    = var.cloud_sql_instance_name
    17→}
    18→
    19→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    20→# Cloud SQL: Databases
    21→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    22→# CrewAI requires three PostgreSQL databases:
    23→# - production: Main application data
    24→# - cable: ActionCable (WebSocket) database for real-time features
    25→# - oauth: OAuth tokens and session data
    26→
    27→resource "google_sql_database" "production" {
    28→  provider = google
    29→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    30→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    31→  name     = var.db_name_production
    32→}
    33→
    34→resource "google_sql_database" "cable" {
    35→  provider = google
    36→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    37→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    38→  name     = var.db_name_cable
    39→}
    40→
    41→resource "google_sql_database" "oauth" {
    42→  provider = google
    43→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    44→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    45→  name     = var.db_name_oauth
    46→}
    47→
    48→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    49→# Cloud SQL: IAM Database User
    50→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    51→# Uses IAM authentication instead of password. The service account authenticates
    52→# via Workload Identity through the Cloud SQL Proxy with --auto-iam-authn flag.
    53→#
    54→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    55→
    56→resource "google_sql_user" "crewai_iam" {
    57→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    58→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    59→  # Cloud SQL IAM users require the email without .gserviceaccount.com suffix
    60→  name = trimsuffix(google_service_account.crewai_platform.email, ".gserviceaccount.com")
    61→  type = "CLOUD_IAM_SERVICE_ACCOUNT"
    62→
    63→  depends_on = [google_service_account.crewai_platform]
    64→}
    65→
    66→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    67→# Cloud SQL Proxy: Kubernetes Deployment
    68→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    69→# Standalone proxy deployment provides shared database connectivity for all pods.
    70→# Uses Workload Identity to authenticate without service account keys.
    71→# All CrewAI pods connect to cloud-sql-proxy:5432 instead of the Cloud SQL IP.
    72→
    73→resource "kubernetes_deployment" "cloud_sql_proxy" {
    74→  metadata {
    75→    name      = "cloud-sql-proxy"
    76→    namespace = local.platform_namespace
    77→    labels    = merge(local.labels, { component = "cloud-sql-proxy" })
    78→  }
    79→
    80→  spec {
    81→    replicas = 1
    82→
    83→    selector {
    84→      match_labels = {
    85→        app       = "cloud-sql-proxy"
    86→        component = "cloud-sql-proxy"
    87→      }
    88→    }
    89→
    90→    template {
    91→      metadata {
    92→        labels = {
    93→          app       = "cloud-sql-proxy"
    94→          component = "cloud-sql-proxy"
    95→        }
    96→      }
    97→
    98→      spec {
    99→        service_account_name = kubernetes_service_account.crewai_platform.metadata[0].name
   100→
   101→        container {
   102→          name  = "cloud-sql-proxy"
   103→          image = "gcr.io/cloud-sql-connectors/cloud-sql-proxy:2.8.2"
   104→
   105→          args = [
   106→            "--structured-logs",
   107→            "--private-ip",
   108→            "--auto-iam-authn",
   109→            "--address=0.0.0.0",
   110→            "--port=5432",
   111→            "--health-check",
   112→            "--http-port=9090",
   113→            "--http-address=0.0.0.0",
   114→            "${var.platform_data_project}:${var.region}:${var.cloud_sql_instance_name}"
   115→          ]
   116→
   117→          port {
   118→            container_port = 5432
   119→            name           = "postgres"
   120→          }
   121→
   122→          port {
   123→            container_port = 9090
   124→            name           = "http"
   125→          }
   126→
   127→          resources {
   128→            requests = {
   129→              cpu    = "100m"
   130→              memory = "128Mi"
   131→            }
   132→            limits = {
   133→              cpu    = "500m"
   134→              memory = "512Mi"
   135→            }
   136→          }
   137→
   138→          # Security hardening: run as non-root with minimal capabilities
   139→          security_context {
   140→            run_as_non_root            = true
   141→            run_as_user                = 65532
   142→            allow_privilege_escalation = false
   143→          }
   144→
   145→          liveness_probe {
   146→            http_get {
   147→              path = "/liveness"
   148→              port = 9090
   149→            }
   150→            initial_delay_seconds = 30
   151→            period_seconds        = 10
   152→          }
   153→
   154→          readiness_probe {
   155→            http_get {
   156→              path = "/readiness"
   157→              port = 9090
   158→            }
   159→            initial_delay_seconds = 10
   160→            period_seconds        = 5
   161→          }
   162→        }
   163→      }
   164→    }
   165→  }
   166→
   167→  # Ignore changes made by GKE Autopilot (annotations, resources, security contexts)
   168→  lifecycle {
   169→    ignore_changes = [
   170→      metadata[0].annotations,
   171→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].container[0].resources,
   172→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].container[0].security_context,
   173→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].security_context,
   174→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].toleration,
   175→    ]
   176→  }
   177→
   178→  depends_on = [kubernetes_namespace.crewai_platform]
   179→}
   180→
   181→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   182→# Cloud SQL Proxy: Kubernetes Service
   183→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   184→# ClusterIP service exposes the proxy to other pods in the namespace.
   185→# Pods connect to cloud-sql-proxy:5432 as configured in database.yml.
   186→
   187→resource "kubernetes_service" "cloud_sql_proxy" {
   188→  metadata {
   189→    name      = "cloud-sql-proxy"
   190→    namespace = local.platform_namespace
   191→    labels    = merge(local.labels, { component = "cloud-sql-proxy" })
   192→  }
   193→
   194→  spec {
   195→    selector = {
   196→      app       = "cloud-sql-proxy"
   197→      component = "cloud-sql-proxy"
   198→    }
   199→
   200→    port {
   201→      port        = 5432
   202→      target_port = 5432
   203→      name        = "postgres"
   204→    }
   205→
   206→    type = "ClusterIP"
   207→  }
   208→
   209→  depends_on = [kubernetes_namespace.crewai_platform]
   210→}
   211→
   212→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   213→# Postgres Password: From Secret Manager
   214→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   215→# The postgres superuser password is stored in the security project.
   216→# Required for running GRANT commands that IAM users cannot execute.
   217→
   218→data "google_secret_manager_secret_version" "postgres_password" {
   219→  project = var.security_project_id
   220→  secret=[REDACTED]"
   221→}
   222→
   223→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   224→# Database Permissions Job
   225→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   226→# Runs as postgres superuser to:
   227→# 1. Create required PostgreSQL extensions (pgcrypto, citext, etc.)
   228→# 2. Grant schema permissions to the IAM service account user
   229→#
   230→# This is required because GCP IAM roles (Cloud SQL Client, Instance User)
   231→# only provide connection access, not PostgreSQL-level permissions.
   232→#
   233→# The job hash ensures it re-runs when databases are recreated.
   234→
   235→resource "kubernetes_job" "db_permissions" {
   236→  metadata {
   237→    name      = "db-permissions-${substr(sha256(join(",", [google_sql_database.production.id, google_sql_database.cable.id, google_sql_database.oauth.id])), 0, 8)}"
   238→    namespace = local.platform_namespace
   239→    labels    = merge(local.labels, { component = "db-permissions" })
   240→  }
   241→
   242→  spec {
   243→    ttl_seconds_after_finished = 300
   244→    backoff_limit              = 3
   245→
   246→    template {
   247→      metadata {
   248→        labels = merge(local.labels, { component = "db-permissions" })
   249→      }
   250→
   251→      spec {
   252→        restart_policy = "Never"
   253→
   254→        container {
   255→          name  = "psql"
   256→          image = "postgres:15-alpine"
   257→
   258→          env {
   259→            name  = "PGPASSWORD"
   260→            value = data.google_secret_manager_secret_version.postgres_password.secret_data
   261→          }
   262→
   263→          env {
   264→            name  = "PGHOST"
   265→            value = "cloud-sql-proxy"
   266→          }
   267→
   268→          env {
   269→            name  = "PGUSER"
   270→            value = "postgres"
   271→          }
   272→
   273→          env {
   274→            name  = "IAM_USER"
   275→            value = "crewai-platform@${var.gke_project_id}.iam"
   276→          }
   277→
   278→          command = ["/bin/sh", "-c"]
   279→          args = [<<-EOT
   280→            for DB in ${var.db_name_production} ${var.db_name_cable} ${var.db_name_oauth}; do
   281→              echo "Setting up database: $DB"
   282→              psql -d "$DB" -c "
   283→                -- Create required extensions (Rails migrations need these)
   284→                CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pgcrypto;
   285→                CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS citext;
   286→                CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS hstore;
   287→                CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm;
   288→
   289→                -- Grant schema permissions to IAM user
   290→                GRANT ALL ON SCHEMA public TO \"$IAM_USER\";
   291→                GRANT ALL PRIVILEGES ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public TO \"$IAM_USER\";
   292→                GRANT ALL PRIVILEGES ON ALL SEQUENCES IN SCHEMA public TO \"$IAM_USER\";
   293→                ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public GRANT ALL ON TABLES TO \"$IAM_USER\";
   294→                ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public GRANT ALL ON SEQUENCES TO \"$IAM_USER\";
   295→              "
   296→            done
   297→            echo "All databases configured successfully"
   298→          EOT
   299→          ]
   300→        }
   301→      }
   302→    }
   303→  }
   304→
   305→  wait_for_completion = true
   306→
   307→  timeouts {
   308→    create = "5m"
   309→  }
   310→
   311→  # Ignore changes made by GKE Autopilot (annotations, resources, security contexts)
   312→  lifecycle {
   313→    ignore_changes = [
   314→      metadata[0].annotations,
   315→      spec[0].selector,
   316→      spec[0].template[0].metadata[0].annotations,
   317→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].container[0].resources,
   318→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].container[0].security_context,
   319→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].security_context,
   320→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].toleration,
   321→    ]
   322→  }
   323→
   324→  depends_on = [
   325→    google_sql_database.production,
   326→    google_sql_database.cable,
   327→    google_sql_database.oauth,
   328→    kubernetes_deployment.cloud_sql_proxy,
   329→    kubernetes_service.cloud_sql_proxy,
   330→  ]
   331→}
   332→

> DEVELOPER

would it be better to move the cloud-sql-proxy into the helm chart managed by argo?

> AGENT

Good question. Here are the trade-offs:

**Keep in Terraform (current):**
- ✅ Proxy exists before ArgoCD sync (migrations need it)
- ✅ Co-located with database, IAM, and Workload Identity config
- ✅ Terraform's db-permissions job depends on it
- ❌ Two systems managing K8s resources in same namespace
- ❌ Requires `terraform apply` before ArgoCD works

**Move to Helm chart:**
- ✅ Single source of truth (GitOps via ArgoCD)
- ✅ All K8s resources visible in git
- ✅ No Terraform dependency for deployments
- ❌ Need to pass Cloud SQL connection string via values
- ❌ Must use sync waves to ensure proxy is up before migrations

**My recommendation:** Move it to Helm. The proxy is a runtime dependency, not infrastructure. It should deploy with the app.

The pattern would be:
- **Terraform**: GCP resources (databases, IAM, service accounts, secrets)
- **Helm/ArgoCD**: K8s resources (deployments, services, jobs)

This is cleaner separation. Want me to create a cloud-sql-proxy template in the local Helm chart and remove it from Terraform?

> DEVELOPER

ok, move it to helm, and make sure it is in the proper sync waves

> AGENT

I'll create the cloud-sql-proxy Helm templates with proper sync waves. The order will be:
- Wave -30: cloud-sql-proxy (deployment + service)
- Wave -25: externalsecret-postgres
- Wave -20: job-db-permissions
- Wave -15: configmap-database

  "content": "{{- /*\nCloud SQL Proxy deployment and service.\nProvides secure database connectivity for all pods via Workload Identity.\nDeployed at wave -30 to ensure it's ready before db-permissions job.\n*/ -}}\n{{- if .Values.cloudSqlProxy.enabled }}\n---\napiVersion: apps/v1\nkind: Deployment\nmetadata:\n  name: cloud-sql-proxy\n  namespace: {{ .Release.Namespace }}\n  labels:\n    {{- include \"crewai-gateway.labels\" . | nindent 4 }}\n    app.kubernetes.io/component: cloud-sql-proxy\n  annotations:\n    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: \"-30\"\nspec:\n  replicas: {{ .Values.cloudSqlProxy.replicas | default 1 }}\n  selector:\n    matchLabels:\n      app: cloud-sql-proxy\n      app.kubernetes.io/component: cloud-sql-proxy\n  template:\n    metadata:\n      labels:\n        app: cloud-sql-proxy\n        app.kubernetes.io/component: cloud-sql-proxy\n    spec:\n      serviceAccountName: {{ .Values.serviceAccount }}\n      containers:\n        - name: cloud-sql-proxy\n          image: {{ .Values.cloudSqlProxy.image | default \"gcr.io/cloud-sql-connectors/cloud-sql-proxy:2.8.2\" }}\n          args:\n            - \"--structured-logs\"\n            - \"--private-ip\"\n            - \"--auto-iam-authn\"\n            - \"--address=0.0.0.0\"\n            - \"--port=5432\"\n            - \"--health-check\"\n            - \"--http-port=9090\"\n            - \"--http-address=0.0.0.0\"\n            - \"{{ .Values.cloudSqlProxy.connectionName }}\"\n          ports:\n            - containerPort: 5432\n              name: postgres\n            - containerPort: 9090\n              name: http\n          resources:\n            requests:\n              cpu: {{ .Values.cloudSqlProxy.resources.requests.cpu | default \"100m\" }}\n              memory: {{ .Values.cloudSqlProxy.resources.requests.memory | default \"128Mi\" }}\n            limits:\n              cpu: {{ .Values.cloudSqlProxy.resources.limits.cpu | default \"500m\" }}\n              memory: {{ .Values.cloudSqlProxy.resources.limits.memory | default \"512Mi\" }}\n          securityContext:\n            runAsNonRoot: true\n            runAsUser: 65532\n            allowPrivilegeEscalation: false\n          livenessProbe:\n            httpGet:\n              path: /liveness\n              port: 9090\n            initialDelaySeconds: 30\n            periodSeconds: 10\n          readinessProbe:\n            httpGet:\n              path: /readiness\n              port: 9090\n            initialDelaySeconds: 10\n            periodSeconds: 5\n---\napiVersion: v1\nkind: Service\nmetadata:\n  name: cloud-sql-proxy\n  namespace: {{ .Release.Namespace }}\n  labels:\n    {{- include \"crewai-gateway.labels\" . | nindent 4 }}\n    app.kubernetes.io/component: cloud-sql-proxy\n  annotations:\n    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: \"-30\"\nspec:\n  selector:\n    app: cloud-sql-proxy\n    app.kubernetes.io/component: cloud-sql-proxy\n  ports:\n    - port: 5432\n      targetPort: 5432\n      name: postgres\n  type: ClusterIP\n{{- end }}\n"
}

     1→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     2→# Cloud SQL Database
     3→#
     4→# Databases and user on the shared platform Cloud SQL instance.
     5→# Cloud SQL Proxy deployment for secure database access from pods.
     6→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     7→
     8→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     9→# Data Source: Existing Cloud SQL Instance
    10→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    11→# The Cloud SQL instance is managed at the platform level, not per-service.
    12→# This data source retrieves the instance details for database creation.
    13→
    14→data "google_sql_database_instance" "platform" {
    15→  project = var.platform_data_project
    16→  name    = var.cloud_sql_instance_name
    17→}
    18→
    19→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    20→# Cloud SQL: Databases
    21→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    22→# CrewAI requires three PostgreSQL databases:
    23→# - production: Main application data
    24→# - cable: ActionCable (WebSocket) database for real-time features
    25→# - oauth: OAuth tokens and session data
    26→
    27→resource "google_sql_database" "production" {
    28→  provider = google
    29→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    30→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    31→  name     = var.db_name_production
    32→}
    33→
    34→resource "google_sql_database" "cable" {
    35→  provider = google
    36→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    37→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    38→  name     = var.db_name_cable
    39→}
    40→
    41→resource "google_sql_database" "oauth" {
    42→  provider = google
    43→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    44→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    45→  name     = var.db_name_oauth
    46→}
    47→
    48→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    49→# Cloud SQL: IAM Database User
    50→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    51→# Uses IAM authentication instead of password. The service account authenticates
    52→# via Workload Identity through the Cloud SQL Proxy with --auto-iam-authn flag.
    53→#
    54→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    55→
    56→resource "google_sql_user" "crewai_iam" {
    57→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    58→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    59→  # Cloud SQL IAM users require the email without .gserviceaccount.com suffix
    60→  name = trimsuffix(google_service_account.crewai_platform.email, ".gserviceaccount.com")
    61→  type = "CLOUD_IAM_SERVICE_ACCOUNT"
    62→
    63→  depends_on = [google_service_account.crewai_platform]
    64→}
    65→
    66→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    67→# Cloud SQL Proxy: Kubernetes Deployment
    68→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    69→# Standalone proxy deployment provides shared database connectivity for all pods.
    70→# Uses Workload Identity to authenticate without service account keys.
    71→# All CrewAI pods connect to cloud-sql-proxy:5432 instead of the Cloud SQL IP.
    72→
    73→resource "kubernetes_deployment" "cloud_sql_proxy" {
    74→  metadata {
    75→    name      = "cloud-sql-proxy"
    76→    namespace = local.platform_namespace
    77→    labels    = merge(local.labels, { component = "cloud-sql-proxy" })
    78→  }
    79→
    80→  spec {
    81→    replicas = 1
    82→
    83→    selector {
    84→      match_labels = {
    85→        app       = "cloud-sql-proxy"
    86→        component = "cloud-sql-proxy"
    87→      }
    88→    }
    89→
    90→    template {
    91→      metadata {
    92→        labels = {
    93→          app       = "cloud-sql-proxy"
    94→          component = "cloud-sql-proxy"
    95→        }
    96→      }
    97→
    98→      spec {
    99→        service_account_name = kubernetes_service_account.crewai_platform.metadata[0].name
   100→
   101→        container {
   102→          name  = "cloud-sql-proxy"
   103→          image = "gcr.io/cloud-sql-connectors/cloud-sql-proxy:2.8.2"
   104→
   105→          args = [
   106→            "--structured-logs",
   107→            "--private-ip",
   108→            "--auto-iam-authn",
   109→            "--address=0.0.0.0",
   110→            "--port=5432",
   111→            "--health-check",
   112→            "--http-port=9090",
   113→            "--http-address=0.0.0.0",
   114→            "${var.platform_data_project}:${var.region}:${var.cloud_sql_instance_name}"
   115→          ]
   116→
   117→          port {
   118→            container_port = 5432
   119→            name           = "postgres"
   120→          }
   121→
   122→          port {
   123→            container_port = 9090
   124→            name           = "http"
   125→          }
   126→
   127→          resources {
   128→            requests = {
   129→              cpu    = "100m"
   130→              memory = "128Mi"
   131→            }
   132→            limits = {
   133→              cpu    = "500m"
   134→              memory = "512Mi"
   135→            }
   136→          }
   137→
   138→          # Security hardening: run as non-root with minimal capabilities
   139→          security_context {
   140→            run_as_non_root            = true
   141→            run_as_user                = 65532
   142→            allow_privilege_escalation = false
   143→          }
   144→
   145→          liveness_probe {
   146→            http_get {
   147→              path = "/liveness"
   148→              port = 9090
   149→            }
   150→            initial_delay_seconds = 30
   151→            period_seconds        = 10
   152→          }
   153→
   154→          readiness_probe {
   155→            http_get {
   156→              path = "/readiness"
   157→              port = 9090
   158→            }
   159→            initial_delay_seconds = 10
   160→            period_seconds        = 5
   161→          }
   162→        }
   163→      }
   164→    }
   165→  }
   166→
   167→  # Ignore changes made by GKE Autopilot (annotations, resources, security contexts)
   168→  lifecycle {
   169→    ignore_changes = [
   170→      metadata[0].annotations,
   171→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].container[0].resources,
   172→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].container[0].security_context,
   173→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].security_context,
   174→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].toleration,
   175→    ]
   176→  }
   177→
   178→  depends_on = [kubernetes_namespace.crewai_platform]
   179→}
   180→
   181→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   182→# Cloud SQL Proxy: Kubernetes Service
   183→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   184→# ClusterIP service exposes the proxy to other pods in the namespace.
   185→# Pods connect to cloud-sql-proxy:5432 as configured in database.yml.
   186→
   187→resource "kubernetes_service" "cloud_sql_proxy" {
   188→  metadata {
   189→    name      = "cloud-sql-proxy"
   190→    namespace = local.platform_namespace
   191→    labels    = merge(local.labels, { component = "cloud-sql-proxy" })
   192→  }
   193→
   194→  spec {
   195→    selector = {
   196→      app       = "cloud-sql-proxy"
   197→      component = "cloud-sql-proxy"
   198→    }
   199→
   200→    port {
   201→      port        = 5432
   202→      target_port = 5432
   203→      name        = "postgres"
   204→    }
   205→
   206→    type = "ClusterIP"
   207→  }
   208→
   209→  depends_on = [kubernetes_namespace.crewai_platform]
   210→}
   211→
   212→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   213→# Postgres Password: From Secret Manager
   214→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   215→# The postgres superuser password is stored in the security project.
   216→# Required for running GRANT commands that IAM users cannot execute.
   217→
   218→data "google_secret_manager_secret_version" "postgres_password" {
   219→  project = var.security_project_id
   220→  secret=[REDACTED]"
   221→}
   222→
   223→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   224→# Database Permissions Job
   225→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   226→# Runs as postgres superuser to:
   227→# 1. Create required PostgreSQL extensions (pgcrypto, citext, etc.)
   228→# 2. Grant schema permissions to the IAM service account user
   229→#
   230→# This is required because GCP IAM roles (Cloud SQL Client, Instance User)
   231→# only provide connection access, not PostgreSQL-level permissions.
   232→#
   233→# The job hash ensures it re-runs when databases are recreated.
   234→
   235→resource "kubernetes_job" "db_permissions" {
   236→  metadata {
   237→    name      = "db-permissions-${substr(sha256(join(",", [google_sql_database.production.id, google_sql_database.cable.id, google_sql_database.oauth.id])), 0, 8)}"
   238→    namespace = local.platform_namespace
   239→    labels    = merge(local.labels, { component = "db-permissions" })
   240→  }
   241→
   242→  spec {
   243→    ttl_seconds_after_finished = 300
   244→    backoff_limit              = 3
   245→
   246→    template {
   247→      metadata {
   248→        labels = merge(local.labels, { component = "db-permissions" })
   249→      }
   250→
   251→      spec {
   252→        restart_policy = "Never"
   253→
   254→        container {
   255→          name  = "psql"
   256→          image = "postgres:15-alpine"
   257→
   258→          env {
   259→            name  = "PGPASSWORD"
   260→            value = data.google_secret_manager_secret_version.postgres_password.secret_data
   261→          }
   262→
   263→          env {
   264→            name  = "PGHOST"
   265→            value = "cloud-sql-proxy"
   266→          }
   267→
   268→          env {
   269→            name  = "PGUSER"
   270→            value = "postgres"
   271→          }
   272→
   273→          env {
   274→            name  = "IAM_USER"
   275→            value = "crewai-platform@${var.gke_project_id}.iam"
   276→          }
   277→
   278→          command = ["/bin/sh", "-c"]
   279→          args = [<<-EOT
   280→            for DB in ${var.db_name_production} ${var.db_name_cable} ${var.db_name_oauth}; do
   281→              echo "Setting up database: $DB"
   282→              psql -d "$DB" -c "
   283→                -- Create required extensions (Rails migrations need these)
   284→                CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pgcrypto;
   285→                CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS citext;
   286→                CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS hstore;
   287→                CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS pg_trgm;
   288→
   289→                -- Grant schema permissions to IAM user
   290→                GRANT ALL ON SCHEMA public TO \"$IAM_USER\";
   291→                GRANT ALL PRIVILEGES ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA public TO \"$IAM_USER\";
   292→                GRANT ALL PRIVILEGES ON ALL SEQUENCES IN SCHEMA public TO \"$IAM_USER\";
   293→                ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public GRANT ALL ON TABLES TO \"$IAM_USER\";
   294→                ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA public GRANT ALL ON SEQUENCES TO \"$IAM_USER\";
   295→              "
   296→            done
   297→            echo "All databases configured successfully"
   298→          EOT
   299→          ]
   300→        }
   301→      }
   302→    }
   303→  }
   304→
   305→  wait_for_completion = true
   306→
   307→  timeouts {
   308→    create = "5m"
   309→  }
   310→
   311→  # Ignore changes made by GKE Autopilot (annotations, resources, security contexts)
   312→  lifecycle {
   313→    ignore_changes = [
   314→      metadata[0].annotations,
   315→      spec[0].selector,
   316→      spec[0].template[0].metadata[0].annotations,
   317→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].container[0].resources,
   318→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].container[0].security_context,
   319→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].security_context,
   320→      spec[0].template[0].spec[0].toleration,
   321→    ]
   322→  }
   323→
   324→  depends_on = [
   325→    google_sql_database.production,
   326→    google_sql_database.cable,
   327→    google_sql_database.oauth,
   328→    kubernetes_deployment.cloud_sql_proxy,
   329→    kubernetes_service.cloud_sql_proxy,
   330→  ]
   331→}
   332→

{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform/database.tf",
  "content": "# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Cloud SQL Database\n#\n# Databases and user on the shared platform Cloud SQL instance.\n# Cloud SQL Proxy is deployed via Helm chart (not Terraform).\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Data Source: Existing Cloud SQL Instance\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# The Cloud SQL instance is managed at the platform level, not per-service.\n# This data source retrieves the instance details for database creation.\n\ndata \"google_sql_database_instance\" \"platform\" {\n  project = var.platform_data_project\n  name    = var.cloud_sql_instance_name\n}\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Cloud SQL: Databases\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# CrewAI requires three PostgreSQL databases:\n# - production: Main application data\n# - cable: ActionCable (WebSocket) database for real-time features\n# - oauth: OAuth tokens and session data\n\nresource \"google_sql_database\" \"production\" {\n  provider = google\n  project  = var.platform_data_project\n  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name\n  name     = var.db_name_production\n}\n\nresource \"google_sql_database\" \"cable\" {\n  provider = google\n  project  = var.platform_data_project\n  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name\n  name     = var.db_name_cable\n}\n\nresource \"google_sql_database\" \"oauth\" {\n  provider = google\n  project  = var.platform_data_project\n  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name\n  name     = var.db_name_oauth\n}\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Cloud SQL: IAM Database User\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Uses IAM authentication instead of password. The service account authenticates\n# via Workload Identity through the Cloud SQL Proxy with --auto-iam-authn flag.\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nresource \"google_sql_user\" \"crewai_iam\" {\n  project  = var.platform_data_project\n  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name\n  # Cloud SQL IAM users require the email without .gserviceaccount.com suffix\n  name = trimsuffix(google_service_account.crewai_platform.email, \".gserviceaccount.com\")\n  type = \"CLOUD_IAM_SERVICE_ACCOUNT\"\n\n  depends_on = [google_service_account.crewai_platform]\n}\n"
}

Done. Terraform removed the 3 resources:
- `kubernetes_deployment.cloud_sql_proxy`
- `kubernetes_service.cloud_sql_proxy`  
- `kubernetes_job.db_permissions`

ArgoCD will now deploy the cloud-sql-proxy via the Helm chart at sync wave -30. The sync order is:

| Wave | Resource |
|------|----------|
| -30 | cloud-sql-proxy deployment + service |
| -25 | externalsecret-postgres |
| -20 | job-db-permissions |
| -15 | configmap-database |
| 0 | main application |

Trigger an ArgoCD sync to deploy the new Helm-managed cloud-sql-proxy.

> AGENT

The issue is that sync waves only work within the same phase. PreSync hooks all run before regular resources deploy. The cloud-sql-proxy needs to be a PreSync hook too.

Let me fix this:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_014Nti39ZYM41xaFTohUtN6m
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/helm/crewai/templates/cloud-sql-proxy.yaml",
  "old_string": "{{- /*\nCloud SQL Proxy deployment and service.\nProvides secure database connectivity for all pods via Workload Identity.\nDeployed at wave -30 to ensure it's ready before db-permissions job.\n*/ -}}\n{{- if .Values.cloudSqlProxy.enabled }}\n---\napiVersion: apps/v1\nkind: Deployment\nmetadata:\n  name: cloud-sql-proxy\n  namespace: {{ .Release.Namespace }}\n  labels:\n    {{- include \"crewai-gateway.labels\" . | nindent 4 }}\n    app.kubernetes.io/component: cloud-sql-proxy\n  annotations:\n    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: \"-30\"",
  "new_string": "{{- /*\nCloud SQL Proxy deployment and service.\nProvides secure database connectivity for all pods via Workload Identity.\nNOT a hook - deploys as regular resource so it persists across syncs.\nUses sync wave -5 to deploy early in the main sync phase.\n*/ -}}\n{{- if .Values.cloudSqlProxy.enabled }}\n---\napiVersion: apps/v1\nkind: Deployment\nmetadata:\n  name: cloud-sql-proxy\n  namespace: {{ .Release.Namespace }}\n  labels:\n    {{- include \"crewai-gateway.labels\" . | nindent 4 }}\n    app.kubernetes.io/component: cloud-sql-proxy\n  annotations:\n    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: \"-5\""
}
```

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01E23PifUQxuBpSG2RiG8UXJ
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/helm/crewai/templates/cloud-sql-proxy.yaml",
  "old_string": "{{- /*\nCloud SQL Proxy deployment and service.\nProvides secure database connectivity for all pods via Workload Identity.\nNOT a hook - deploys as regular resource so it persists across syncs.\nUses sync wave -5 to deploy early in the main sync phase.\n*/ -}}\n{{- if .Values.cloudSqlProxy.enabled }}\n---\napiVersion: apps/v1\nkind: Deployment\nmetadata:\n  name: cloud-sql-proxy\n  namespace: {{ .Release.Namespace }}\n  labels:\n    {{- include \"crewai-gateway.labels\" . | nindent 4 }}\n    app.kubernetes.io/component: cloud-sql-proxy\n  annotations:\n    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: \"-5\"",
  "new_string": "{{- /*\nCloud SQL Proxy deployment and service.\nProvides secure database connectivity for all pods via Workload Identity.\nPreSync hook at wave -30 ensures it's ready before db-permissions job.\nBeforeHookCreation policy keeps it running between syncs.\n*/ -}}\n{{- if .Values.cloudSqlProxy.enabled }}\n---\napiVersion: apps/v1\nkind: Deployment\nmetadata:\n  name: cloud-sql-proxy\n  namespace: {{ .Release.Namespace }}\n  labels:\n    {{- include \"crewai-gateway.labels\" . | nindent 4 }}\n    app.kubernetes.io/component: cloud-sql-proxy\n  annotations:\n    argocd.argoproj.io/hook: PreSync\n    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: \"-30\"\n    argocd.argoproj.io/hook-delete-policy: BeforeHookCreation"
}

## CrewAI Deployment Architecture Summary

Based on my review of the kix-services-crewai repository, here is a comprehensive summary of the current architecture:

---

## 1. ArgoCD Application Structure (Multi-Source Pattern)

### Current Implementation

All three environments (dev, QA, prod) use a **multi-source ArgoCD Application** with 2-3 sources:

**Dev Environment** (`environments/dev/argocd/application.yaml`):
```yaml
sources:
  - repoURL: registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable        # Source 1: OCI chart
    chart: crewai-platform
    targetRevision: 0.3.13
    helm:
      releaseName: crewai-platform
      valueFiles:
        - $values/environments/dev/helm/crewai/values.yaml
  
  - repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai
    path: environments/dev/helm/crewai                # Source 2: Local gateway chart
    helm:
      releaseName: crewai-gateway
      valueFiles:
        - values.yaml
  
  - repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai
    ref: values                                        # Source 3: Git reference for $values
```

**QA/Prod Environments** (simplified - 2 sources):
```yaml
sources:
  - repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable   # OCI chart only
    chart: crewai-platform
    targetRevision: 0.3.10
    helm:
      valueFiles:
        - $values/environments/{env}/helm/values.yaml
  
  - repoURL: https://github.com/...                     # Values reference
    ref: values
```

**Key Characteristics:**
- **Dev has 2 Helm releases**: upstream OCI chart (crewai-platform) + local chart (crewai-gateway)
- **QA/Prod have 1 Helm release**: upstream OCI chart only
- **Architecture divergence**: Dev cannot validate what runs in production because it uses fundamentally different deployments
- **Sync options**: CreateNamespace=true, Validate=true, ServerSideApply=true, RespectIgnoreDifferences=true
- **Automated sync**: Enabled in dev/qa with prune and selfHeal; disabled in prod

---

## 2. Terraform vs Helm: Responsibility Separation

### Terraform Manages (All Environments)

From `environments/{env}/terraform/main.tf` and related files:

**Infrastructure Layer:**
- GCP service accounts and IAM bindings
- Kubernetes namespaces and resource quotas
- Cloud SQL databases and users (data source lookup)
- GCS buckets for data and logs
- Artifact Registry for crew image builds
- HMAC keys for S3-compatible GCS access
- Random password generation (DB, Rails SECRET_KEY_BASE, keys)

**Secret Storage:**
- **GCP Secret Manager secrets** (single-secret-per-call pattern):
  - `crewai-rails-secrets-{env}` (SECRET_KEY_BASE)
  - `crewai-gcs-credentials-{env}` (AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID, AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY)
  - `crewai-workos-credentials-{env}` (OAuth credentials)

**Kubernetes Resources Created by Terraform:**
- **In Dev Only**:
  - Cloud SQL Proxy deployment and service (with PreSync hook, wave -30)
  - Kubernetes manifest for ExternalSecret (references GCP SM secrets)
  - Network policies
  - Namespace-scoped SecretStore (for ESO)
  
- **In All Environments**:
  - Kubernetes service accounts (via `gke-config` module)
  - Pod security standards
  - Resource quotas and limits

### Helm Manages (Upstream OCI Chart)

**Application Workloads:**
- Web deployment (Rails application)
- Worker deployment (background jobs)
- Buildkit deployment (container image builder for crew workloads)
- Replicated SDK deployment (license management)
- Scheduler, API, and other services

**Environment-Specific Configuration via `values.yaml`:**
- Component replicas and resource requests/limits
- Service configurations and health probes
- Security contexts and pod security
- Autoscaling policies (HPA for prod)
- Database configuration
- Storage (GCS) configuration
- Application environment variables
- Image registry and pull secrets

---

## 3. External Secrets Operator (ESO) Pattern

### Current Implementation (Dev Only)

**Terraform creates ExternalSecret manifests** (in `environments/dev/terraform/secrets.tf`):

```hcl
resource "kubernetes_manifest" "external_secret_crewai_credentials" {
  manifest = {
    apiVersion = "external-secrets.io/v1beta1"
    kind       = "ExternalSecret"
    metadata = {
      name      = "crewai-credentials"
      namespace = local.platform_namespace
    }
    spec = {
      refreshInterval = "1h"
      secretStoreRef = {
        kind = "ClusterSecretStore"
        name = "gcp-secret-manager"                    # Platform-managed
      }
      target = {
        name           = "crewai-secrets"
        creationPolicy = "Owner"
      }
      data = [
        { secretKey = "SECRET_KEY_BASE", remoteRef = { key = "crewai-rails-secrets-{env}", property = "secret_key_base" } },
        { secretKey = "AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID", remoteRef = { ... } },
        { secretKey = "AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY", remoteRef = { ... } },
        { secretKey = "WORKOS_CLIENT_ID", remoteRef = { ... } },
        { secretKey = "WORKOS_API_KEY", remoteRef = { ... } },
        { secretKey = "WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD", remoteRef = { ... } }
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

**How It Works:**
1. Terraform creates GCP Secret Manager secrets with the actual values
2. Terraform creates a Kubernetes ExternalSecret manifest that references the ClusterSecretStore
3. ESO controller (platform-managed) watches ExternalSecret and syncs data from GCP SM → K8s secret
4. The resulting K8s secret `crewai-secrets` is injected into pods via `envFrom`

**Architecture:**
```
GCP Secret Manager (managed by Terraform)
    ↓
ExternalSecret manifest (created by Terraform)
    ↓
ClusterSecretStore "gcp-secret-manager" (platform-managed, already exists)
    ↓
K8s Secret "crewai-secrets" (created by ESO controller)
    ↓
Pods consume via: envFrom: [{secretRef: {name: "crewai-secrets"}}]
```

### Dev-Only Helm ExternalSecret Template

Additionally, dev has a **Helm-templated ExternalSecret** (`templates/externalsecret-postgres.yaml`) for postgres superuser credentials:

```yaml
apiVersion: external-secrets.io/v1beta1
kind: ExternalSecret
metadata:
  name: crewai-gateway-postgres
  annotations:
    argocd.argoproj.io/hook: PreSync
    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: "-25"
spec:
  secretStoreRef:
    kind: ClusterSecretStore
    name: gcp-secret-manager
  target:
    name: postgres-credentials
  data:
    - secretKey: password
      remoteRef:
        key: cloudsql-postgres-password
```

**Purpose:** Fetches postgres superuser password needed by the db-permissions job before migrations run.

---

## 4. Cloud SQL Proxy Setup

### Dev: Standalone Service (PreSync Hook)

**Helm template** (`templates/cloud-sql-proxy.yaml`):
```yaml
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  annotations:
    argocd.argoproj.io/hook: PreSync
    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: "-30"
    argocd.argoproj.io/hook-delete-policy: BeforeHookCreation
spec:
  containers:
    - name: cloud-sql-proxy
      image: gcr.io/cloud-sql-connectors/cloud-sql-proxy:2.8.2
      args:
        - "--structured-logs"
        - "--private-ip"
        - "--auto-iam-authn"              # IAM authentication (dev-specific)
        - "--address=0.0.0.0"
        - "--port=5432"
        - "--health-check"
        - "--http-port=9090"
        - "kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data:europe-west1:kd-ix-eur-dev-cloudsql-platform"
```

**Configuration:**
- **Sync wave:** `-30` (runs before all other resources)
- **Hook delete policy:** `BeforeHookCreation` (keeps running after PreSync completes)
- **Authentication:** IAM authentication via Workload Identity
- **Database access:** All pods connect via `cloud-sql-proxy:5432` hostname
- **Service resource:** Also created with PreSync hook, wave -30

### QA/Prod: Sidecar Container (Within Pod)

The upstream Helm chart deploys cloud-sql-proxy as an extra container within the web, worker, and buildkit pods:

```yaml
extraContainers:
  - name: cloud-sql-proxy
    image: gcr.io/cloud-sql-connectors/cloud-sql-proxy:2.8.2
    args:
      - "--structured-logs"
      - "--port=5432"
      - "--http-port=9090"
      - "--http-address=0.0.0.0"
      - "kd-ix-eur-prod-platform-data:europe-west1:kd-ix-eur-prod-cloudsql-platform"
```

**Configuration:**
- **Runs in each pod** alongside the main application container
- **Authentication:** Password-based (different from dev's IAM auth)
- **Database access:** All pods connect via `localhost:5432`

**Key Difference:**
- **Dev:** Standalone service (shared proxy, IAM auth, better resource efficiency)
- **QA/Prod:** Sidecar container (isolated per pod, password auth, simpler but resource-intensive)

---

## 5. Sync Wave Order and PreSync Hooks

### Dev Environment Hook Execution Order

**Wave Sequence (executed sequentially):**

```
-30: Cloud SQL Proxy (PreSync)
     ├── Deployment (BeforeHookCreation policy - stays running)
     └── Service (BeforeHookCreation policy - stays running)
     
-25: ExternalSecret for postgres credentials (PreSync)
     └── Syncs cloudsql-postgres-password from GCP SM to K8s
     
-20: Database permissions job (PreSync)
     ├── Waits for cloud-sql-proxy to be ready
     ├── Uses postgres credentials from wave -25
     ├── Grants schema permissions to IAM user
     ├── Hook delete policy: BeforeHookCreation (deleted before main sync)
     └── TTL: 300s after completion
     
-15: Database config ConfigMap (PreSync)
     ├── ERB template with environment variables
     ├── Hook delete policy: HookSucceeded (deleted after PreSync succeeds)
     └── Reason: Upstream chart creates persistent version in main sync (wave 0)
     
0:   Main application deployment (main sync)
     ├── Web deployment (from OCI chart)
     ├── Worker deployment
     ├── Buildkit deployment
     ├── Replicated SDK
     ├── Network policies (from local chart)
     ├── Gateway resources (from local chart)
     └── HTTPRoutes and health check policies
```

**Hook Delete Policies Used:**

| Resource | Policy | Reason |
|----------|--------|--------|
| cloud-sql-proxy | BeforeHookCreation | Keep running as shared infrastructure |
| db-permissions job | BeforeHookCreation | Clean up after success, before main sync |
| database-config ConfigMap | HookSucceeded | Delete after PreSync completes (upstream creates persistent version) |
| externalsecret-postgres | (default) | No special cleanup |

**Sync Wave Notes:**
- PreSync hooks (-30 to -15) must complete before main sync (wave 0)
- Each wave waits for previous wave to complete (strict ordering)
- `HookSucceeded` ensures temporary resources don't conflict with permanent ones from main chart

---

## 6. Key Architecture Differences Across Environments

| Aspect | Dev | QA | Prod |
|--------|-----|-----|------|
| **ArgoCD Sources** | 3 (OCI + local + values) | 2 (OCI + values) | 2 (OCI + values) |
| **Local Helm Chart** | 18 templates (gateway, policies, etc.) | None | None |
| **Cloud SQL Proxy** | Standalone service (PreSync hook) | Sidecar container | Sidecar container |
| **Cloud SQL Auth** | IAM (Workload Identity) | Password | Password |
| **Database Permissions Job** | PreSync hook at wave -20 | Manual/missing | Manual/missing |
| **ESO Pattern** | Terraform creates ExternalSecret | None | None |
| **Network Policies** | Helm-managed (templates) | Not deployed | Not deployed |
| **Gateway API** | Helm-managed (templates) | Not deployed | Not deployed |
| **Secret Creation** | Terraform + ESO | Terraform only | Terraform only |
| **Secrets Refresh** | 1h (ESO watches) | One-time (Terraform) | One-time (Terraform) |
| **Replica Count** | 1 (dev sizing) | Varies | 3-10 (HPA enabled) |
| **Image Registry** | AR mirror (Workload Identity) | CrewAI registry (secret) | CrewAI registry (secret) |
| **Environment Parity** | Different code paths | 99% same as Prod | Reference |

---

## 7. Key Patterns and Decisions

### Single-Secret-Per-Call Pattern (Terraform)

Each GCP Secret Manager secret gets its own module call:
- `secret_rails_secrets` → CrewAI-rails-secrets-{env}
- `secret_gcs_credentials` → crewai-gcs-credentials-{env}
- `secret_workos_credentials` → crewai-workos-credentials-{env}

Each module:
1. Creates the secret in GCP SM
2. Grants access to the service account via IAM
3. Is referenced by ExternalSecret (dev) or directly consumed (QA/Prod)

### Service Account Management

**Created by:** Terraform `gke-config` module (all environments)

**Workload Identity Binding:**
- K8s service account `crewai-platform-sa` → GCP service account `crewai-platform@{project}.iam.gserviceaccount.com`
- Helm values set `serviceAccount.create: false` to prevent chart from creating duplicate

**Service account permissions:**
- Cloud SQL client role (for IAM auth in dev)
- GCS bucket access (HMAC keys)
- Secret Accessor role (for ESO to read GCP SM)
- Artifact Registry access (for crew image builds)

### Database Authentication Strategy

**Dev:** IAM authentication via Cloud SQL Proxy
- Uses service account credentials (Workload Identity)
- No database password needed in K8s secrets
- More secure (no static credentials)
- Connection string: `cloud-sql-proxy:5432` with user `crewai-platform@{project}.iam`

**QA/Prod:** Password authentication
- Static password stored in GCP Secret Manager
- Synced to K8s secret (dev via ESO, QA/Prod via Terraform)
- Simpler initial setup, less secure (password in multiple systems)
- Connection string: `localhost:5432` or `cloud-sql-proxy:5432` with password

---

## 8. Notable Architectural Decisions

### Why Multi-Source in Dev?

The local `crewai-gateway` chart handles resources the upstream chart doesn't manage:
- **Gateway API resources** (HTTPRoute, GatewayPolicy)
- **Network policies** (egress to external services, cloud-sql-proxy)
- **Database migration hooks** (PreSync configuration, sync waves)
- **Replicated SDK** configuration (specific image path)

### Why No Local Chart in QA/Prod?

QA and Prod rely on the upstream OCI chart exclusively because:
1. **No custom networking** - production uses external load balancers managed separately
2. **No database migrations** - handled outside of chart deployment
3. **Manual gateway setup** - not deployed via ArgoCD
4. **Different infrastructure assumptions** - password auth, sidecar proxies

### Why ESO in Dev Only?

ESO enables local secret rotation and refresh without redeployment:
- **Dev needs rapid iteration** - secrets can change without kubectl apply
- **QA/Prod use stable credentials** - applied once via Terraform, rarely change
- **Platform baseline** - ClusterSecretStore is platform-managed (ESO controller already running)

### Why Cloud SQL Proxy Pattern Differs?

**Dev standalone:**
- Reusable across multiple pods
- Better resource efficiency
- Clearer separation of concerns
- Simpler for development/testing

**QA/Prod sidecar:**
- Closer to production database connection pattern
- Each pod has its own connection pool
- Aligns with upstream chart design
- Matches CrewAI Enterprise recommendations

---

## Recommendations for Documentation Updates

The current architecture should be documented as:

1. **Multi-environment deployment model** - Three distinct patterns (not a bug, a feature for different operational needs)
2. **ESO as optional enhancement** - Dev-specific for local iteration; core pattern is Terraform-managed secrets
3. **PreSync hooks for ordering** - Essential for dev's complex initialization (proxy → permissions → config → app)
4. **Cloud SQL Proxy evolution** - Dev prototype with IAM auth; QA/Prod production pattern with password auth
5. **Gateway API as local extension** - Dev adds networking layer; QA/Prod use external infrastructure

### Architecture Summary Diagram

```
Environments:
├── Dev (Full-Featured)
│   ├── Terraform (infrastructure + ESO + local resources)
│   ├── OCI Chart (crewai-platform)
│   ├── Local Chart (crewai-gateway: policies, Gateway API, PreSync hooks)
│   └── ESO (Cloud SQL Proxy at -30, Postgres secret at -25, DB perms at -20, config at -15)
│
├── QA (Simplified Production Preview)
│   ├── Terraform (infrastructure + credentials only)
│   ├── OCI Chart (crewai-platform with sidecar proxy)
│   └── Manual gateway setup (outside ArgoCD)
│
└── Prod (Full Production)
    ├── Terraform (infrastructure + credentials)
    ├── OCI Chart (crewai-platform with sidecar proxy + HPA)
    └── Manual gateway + monitoring setup
```

     1→# CLAUDE.md - CrewAI Platform Service
     2→
     3→**Repository:** kix-services-crewai
     4→**Purpose:** Multi-environment deployment infrastructure for CrewAI Enterprise on GKE
     5→
     6→---
     7→
     8→## Extends Master Standards
     9→
    10→> **IMPORTANT:** This file extends the **master CLAUDE.md** with service-specific guidance.
    11→>
    12→> **Universal standards (security, git workflow, operational rules) are defined in:**
    13→> **[kix-platform-docs/CLAUDE.md](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/blob/main/CLAUDE.md)**
    14→>
    15→> **This file contains:**
    16→> - Repository structure and architecture
    17→> - Terraform module composition patterns
    18→> - Helm values and ArgoCD configuration
    19→> - Environment-specific conventions
    20→> - Key patterns and decisions
    21→
    22→---
    23→
    24→## Repository Overview
    25→
    26→This is the **kix-services-crewai** repository - deployment infrastructure for **CrewAI Enterprise Platform** across dev, qa, and prod environments on Google Kubernetes Engine.
    27→
    28→**Mission:** Deploy and manage CrewAI Enterprise using Terraform for infrastructure and ArgoCD for application delivery, composing remote modules from kix-platform-modules.
    29→
    30→## Repository Structure
    31→
    32→```
    33→kix-services-crewai/
    34→├── environments/
    35→│   ├── dev/
    36→│   │   ├── argocd/
    37→│   │   │   └── application.yaml      # ArgoCD multi-source Application
    38→│   │   ├── helm/
    39→│   │   │   └── values.yaml           # Helm values for OCI chart
    40→│   │   └── terraform/
    41→│   │       ├── backend.tf            # GCS state backend
    42→│   │       ├── main.tf               # Infrastructure composition
    43→│   │       ├── outputs.tf
    44→│   │       ├── variables.tf
    45→│   │       ├── versions.tf
    46→│   │       └── README.md             # Auto-generated terraform-docs
    47→│   ├── qa/                           # Same structure as dev
    48→│   └── prod/                         # Same structure as dev
    49→├── docs/
    50→│   ├── INDEX.md                      # Documentation index
    51→│   ├── SETUP.md                      # Setup instructions
    52→│   ├── QUICK_REFERENCE.md            # Quick reference
    53→│   ├── CICD.md                       # CI/CD architecture
    54→│   ├── CREW_NAMESPACE.md             # Crew namespace docs
    55→│   ├── runbooks/                     # Operational runbooks
    56→│   ├── archive/                      # Historical documents
    57→│   ├── changelog/                    # Change records
    58→│   ├── prps/                         # Product requirements
    59→│   └── tasks/                        # Task breakdowns
    60→├── CLAUDE.md                         # This file
    61→└── README.md                         # Main README
    62→```
    63→
    64→## Architecture
    65→
    66→### Deployment Model
    67→
    68→- **Infrastructure**: Terraform per environment, composing remote modules from [kix-platform-modules](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules)
    69→- **Application**: External OCI Helm chart (`oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable`) deployed via ArgoCD multi-source Application
    70→- **Secrets**: Terraform creates both GCP Secret Manager secrets and K8s secrets; pods consume credentials via `envFrom` secretRef
    71→- **Identity**: GCP service account with Workload Identity binding to K8s service account
    72→
    73→### Environment Layout
    74→
    75→| Environment | GKE Project | Platform Data Project | Cloud SQL Instance |
    76→|------------|-------------|----------------------|-------------------|
    77→| dev | kd-ix-eur-dev-gke | kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data | kd-ix-eur-dev-cloudsql-platform |
    78→| qa | kd-ix-eur-qa-gke | kd-ix-eur-qa-platform-data | kd-ix-eur-qa-cloudsql-platform |
    79→| prod | kd-ix-eur-prod-gke | kd-ix-eur-prod-platform-data | kd-ix-eur-prod-cloudsql-platform |
    80→
    81→### Namespace Pattern
    82→
    83→Each environment has two namespaces:
    84→- `crewai-{env}` - Platform services (web, worker, scheduler, etc.)
    85→- `crewai-crews-{env}` - Crew workload execution (isolated from platform)
    86→
    87→## Key Patterns and Decisions
    88→
    89→### Terraform Module Composition
    90→
    91→Terraform composes remote modules from kix-platform-modules (`?ref=main`) plus inline resources:
    92→
    93→**Remote modules used:**
    94→- `data-services/gcs` - GCS buckets (data + logs)
    95→- `secret-manager` - GCP Secret Manager secrets (one module call per secret)
    96→- `gke-config` - Kubernetes namespaces, resource quotas, network policies, K8s service accounts
    97→
    98→**Inline resources (not in modules):**
    99→- Cloud SQL databases and user on existing instance (data source lookup)
   100→- GCP service account (inline to avoid circular dependency with HMAC keys)
   101→- HMAC keys for S3-compatible GCS access
   102→- IAM bindings (Cloud SQL client, GCS access, Workload Identity)
   103→- Artifact Registry repository for crew container image builds
   104→- Kubernetes secrets for application credential consumption
   105→- Random passwords for DB, SECRET_KEY_BASE, PLATFORM_MASTER_KEY
   106→
   107→### Secret-Manager Module Interface
   108→
   109→The secret-manager module uses a **single-secret-per-call pattern**:
   110→
   111→```hcl
   112→module "secret_db_credentials" {
   113→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
   114→
   115→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
   116→  environment = var.environment
   117→  secret_id   = "crewai-db-credentials-${var.environment}"
   118→  secret_data = jsonencode({
   119→    password = random_password.db_password.result
   120→    host     = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.private_ip_address
   121→    port     = 5432
   122→    database = "crewai_plus_production"
   123→    username = "crewai"
   124→  })
   125→
   126→  accessor_service_accounts = [google_service_account.crewai_platform.email]
   127→  additional_labels         = local.labels
   128→}
   129→```
   130→
   131→Each secret type gets its own module call: `secret_db_credentials`, `secret_gcs_credentials`, `secret_oauth_credentials`, `secret_helm_registry`.
   132→
   133→### Helm Values and Secret Wiring
   134→
   135→Pods receive credentials via `envFrom` referencing the Terraform-managed K8s secret:
   136→
   137→```yaml
   138→envFrom:
   139→  - secretRef:
   140→      name: "crewai-credentials"
   141→```
   142→
   143→The K8s secret `crewai-credentials` contains: `DB_PASSWORD`, `SECRET_KEY_BASE`, `PLATFORM_MASTER_KEY`, `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID`, `WORKOS_API_KEY`.
   144→
   145→### Service Account Management
   146→
   147→**Terraform manages the K8s service account** via the `gke-config` module. Helm values set `serviceAccount.create: false` to avoid conflict:
   148→
   149→```yaml
   150→serviceAccount:
   151→  create: false
   152→  name: "crewai-platform-sa"
   153→```
   154→
   155→### ArgoCD Multi-Source Application
   156→
   157→ArgoCD uses multi-source to pull the OCI chart and git values separately:
   158→
   159→```yaml
   160→sources:
   161→  - repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable
   162→    targetRevision: "0.3.10"
   163→    chart: crewai-enterprise
   164→    helm:
   165→      valueFiles:
   166→        - $values/environments/{env}/helm/values.yaml
   167→  - repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai.git
   168→    targetRevision: main
   169→    ref: values
   170→```
   171→
   172→### Pod Security Standards
   173→
   174→- Dev/QA: `baseline` mode with `enforce`
   175→- Prod: `baseline` mode with `enforce` (required for BuildKit capabilities)
   176→
   177→BuildKit needs capabilities like CHOWN and DAC_OVERRIDE, which are blocked by `restricted` PSS.
   178→
   179→## Terraform State
   180→
   181→State is stored in per-environment GCS buckets with prefix `services/crewai/resources`:
   182→
   183→| Environment | Backend Bucket | Prefix |
   184→|------------|---------------|--------|
   185→| dev | kd-tfstate-dev | services/crewai/resources |
   186→| qa | kd-tfstate-qa | services/crewai/resources |
   187→| prod | kd-tfstate-prod | services/crewai/resources |
   188→
   189→## Validation Commands
   190→
   191→```bash
   192→# Format check
   193→cd environments/{env}/terraform
   194→terraform fmt -check
   195→
   196→# Initialize (no backend for validation)
   197→terraform init -backend=false
   198→
   199→# Validate
   200→terraform validate
   201→
   202→# Lint
   203→tflint
   204→
   205→# Generate docs
   206→terraform-docs markdown table . > README.md
   207→```
   208→
   209→## Common Tasks
   210→
   211→### Adding a New Environment Variable
   212→
   213→1. Add the value source to `main.tf` (variable, random_password, or data source)
   214→2. Add the key to the `kubernetes_secret.crewai_credentials` data block
   215→3. Run `terraform apply` - pods will pick up the new secret via `envFrom`
   216→
   217→### Upgrading CrewAI Version
   218→
   219→1. Update `targetRevision` in `environments/{env}/argocd/application.yaml`
   220→2. Commit and push
   221→3. ArgoCD auto-syncs (dev/qa) or manually sync (prod)
   222→
   223→### Rotating Database Password
   224→
   225→1. Taint the random_password: `terraform taint random_password.db_password`
   226→2. Run `terraform apply` - updates Cloud SQL user, Secret Manager, and K8s secret
   227→
   228→## Mistakes Learned
   229→
   230→### Secret-Manager Module Interface Mismatch
   231→
   232→The `feature/crewai-platform` branch of kix-platform-modules has a different secret-manager interface (multi-credential pattern) than `main` branch (single-secret-per-call). Since terraform references `?ref=main`, always use the main branch interface with individual module calls per secret.
   233→
   234→### Dual Service Account Creation
   235→
   236→Both Helm (`serviceAccount.create: true`) and Terraform (`gke-config` module) can create the same K8s service account. Always set `serviceAccount.create: false` in Helm values since Terraform manages the SA with Workload Identity binding.
   237→
   238→### PSS vs BuildKit
   239→
   240→CrewAI Builder uses BuildKit which requires Linux capabilities blocked by `restricted` Pod Security Standards. Use `baseline` PSS level for all environments.
   241→
   242→## Repository Relationships
   243→
   244→- **[kix-platform-modules](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules)** - Terraform modules consumed by this repo
   245→- **[kix-platform-infrastructure](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-infrastructure)** - Platform-level infrastructure
   246→- **[kix-platform-docs](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs)** - Master CLAUDE.md and platform documentation
   247→
   248→---
   249→
   250→**Document Version:** 1.0
   251→**Last Updated:** February 2026
   252→**Maintained By:** Platform Engineering Team
   253→
